Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `comb_mult_add_16` (precision INT16)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx
- input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
- output ports: result[64]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: none; the block is combinational, and `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.ws.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_mult_add_16 (precision INT16)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx
  • input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.ws under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 10.6617 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 94 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 7.45611e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 381872, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 3.03568e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in pooling: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (pooling's), from a block RAM output through one LUT into the multiply, then five LUTs into a block RAM input; wire 7.96 ns in all, 1.31 of it into the multiply. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 94 tiles (qmult 255 of its 256 element products on 84 tiles, 49 of them full; norm_sub 16 on 5 tiles; sub_activation 16 on 6; pooling's 8 x 8 product on 1); the 256th element product and pooling's 8 x 11 address product share the one tile in two_mult_18x19 mode.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • The block's internal delay is fixed. Soft logic between a block's output and the next register adds to that stage; blocks in series each add their delay plus a wire.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_mult_add_16.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.ws.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 1
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 9.0
MAPPING: ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int} (pooling line 7489, enable_pool ? matrix_size : 8'b1); ay = {5'b0, y} (pooling's 11-bit counter y, line 7610); bx = {21'b0, xk}, where xk is a new 11-bit register in pooling that holds x + k (see PLAN); result[AWIDTH-1:0] = result[10:0] drives the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool` (line 7612).

PLAN:

What is converted

Only pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133, the one mult_add in
assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff: ((matrix_size_int)*y + x + k); (line 7612).
This is the product that sits on the design's slowest stage: its result runs through the +x, +k adders and the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux to raddr_accum0_pool, out of pooling into top, through the buffer_select_pool0 mux (line 6515, raddr_buffer0 = buffer_select_pool0 ? raddr_accum0_pool : ...) into the accumulator buffer RAM's address input — exactly the reported "five LUTs into a block RAM input". One block absorbs the multiply and the whole +x+k sum, so three of those soft levels and their wires leave the critical stage.

Edit to make

  1. In pooling, add an 11-bit register xk that always carries x + k, maintained in the same always @(posedge clk) block as x, y, k (lines 7614-7632), one assignment per existing branch so it updates, holds and resets exactly when x and k do:

    • if(~resetn|~start_pooling): xk <= 0; (matches new x=0, k=0)
    • else if (y == (matrix_size_int-1) & x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k + filter_size_int; (new x=0, new k=k+filter_size_int)
    • else if (x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k; (new x=0, new k=k)
    • else if (start_pooling): xk <= x + k + 1; (new x=x+1, new k=k)
    • implicit else (hold): no assignment, so xk holds, as x and k do.
      All of these are 11-bit assignments, so they truncate mod 2^11 exactly as x<=x+1 and k<=k+filter_size_int do; xk therefore equals (x+k) mod 2^11 in every cycle after the first reset. x and k themselves are kept unchanged — they are still read by the counter logic (lines 7620-7630), so nothing moves inside the block that is read elsewhere.

  2. Instantiate the block in pooling and take its low 11 bits:

      wire [63:0] raddr_accum0_pool_result;
  comb_mult_add_16 u_raddr_accum0_pool_mult (
      .ax({8'b0, matrix_size_int}),
      .ay({5'b0, y}),
      .bx({21'b0, xk}),
      .result(raddr_accum0_pool_result)
  );
  assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling) ? 11'h7ff : raddr_accum0_pool_result[`AWIDTH-1:0];

    The ~resetn|~start_pooling mux stays outside the block, unchanged, so raddr_accum0_pool is still 11'h7ff whenever the original forces it — this is also what makes the added register safe: on every cycle in which xk could be stale the output does not depend on it.

  3. Change nothing else. raddr_accum0_pool keeps its width, its 15-deep delay pipeline (line 7635) and its port behaviour.

Why this is the same value. All operands are unsigned (matrix_size_int 8-bit, y, x, k 11-bit), and the original RHS is evaluated in the 11-bit context of raddr_accum0_pool, i.e. (matrix_size_int*y + x + k) mod 2^11. The block computes matrix_size_int*y + (x+k) exactly (max 255·2047 + 2047 < 2^32, so the 64-bit result never wraps), and taking result[10:0] reduces mod 2^11; since xk ≡ x+k (mod 2^11), the low 11 bits agree. The block is combinational and has no registers, so no reset has to be derived for it. No ASSUMES: is needed.

Candidates left, and why

  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16) and pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (1): pure multiplies, no sum for the block's bx to absorb. Each one converted costs a whole DSP tile (the block's mode takes a tile alone) in place of a quarter of a tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and removes no soft logic, so area rises and delay is unchanged. :8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int) is already a hard multiply whose fan-out is ordinary flip-flops (pool_count0, pool_norm_valid, pool0..15), not the slowest stage, so converting it buys nothing and costs a tile.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1..#16 (16 ids, 256 products): left for two independent reasons. (a) Correctness/shape: in seq_mac the product does not reach the adder directly — mul_out is a saturating 8-bit downcast of the 16-bit product (lines 4937-4949) and only then added to c_flopped by qadd. (saturate(p) + c) mod 256 is not (p + c) mod 2^64 truncated, so the block cannot hold the multiply and that add together; the downcast would have to stay outside and nothing would be absorbed. (b) Capacity/area: one block per product is 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, and even one 16-product chain would be 16 tiles where the baseline uses 4, with 16 blocks in series (16×2.14 + 15 wires ≈ 51 ns) if the chain were fused. Not viable.
  • sub_activation.$mul$...:8509$1681 (16): the product is registered one stage before the sum (slope_applied_data_internal, line 8509, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so fusing means merging those two registers into one — possible, since both update, hold and reset together and neither is read anywhere else, and the 8-bit truncations compose. But it is left out on cost: it removes only 16 eight-bit adders and 16 eight-bit registers while taking 16 dedicated DSP tiles in place of 16 slots that currently share 6 tiles, roughly +10 tiles ≈ +2.5e6 area (+3.4%) against a saving of a fraction of that in soft logic, and it is nowhere near the slowest stage, so delay does not improve. Net objective would rise.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates 1 block, on 1 DSP tile of its own. The 8×11 product leaves the soft-inferred multiplier pool; the tile it shared in two_mult_18x19 mode is freed and its other occupant (the 256th qmult element product) packs into one of the 37 tiles that are not full in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. So the design uses 93 tiles for the remaining 289 inferred multiplies plus 1 block tile: 94 tiles, unchanged against the baseline's 94, and well inside the device's 132. Used logic-block area is therefore flat to slightly below baseline (two 11-bit adders removed, one 11-bit register added), and routed wirelength is essentially unchanged; the gain in the objective comes from delay.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains more than a single block, so the block contribution to any stage is 2.14 ns, against the 10.6617 ns baseline critical path.

Soft logic that leaves, and which stage. The +x and +k 11-bit adders leave the stage that runs from the matrix_size/y/x/k registers through the multiply to the accumulator-buffer RAM address input — that stage is the design's slowest (10.6617 ns, the reported pooling path: BRAM-side start, one LUT into the multiply, five LUTs into a block RAM input). After the rewrite that stage contains, at most, one LUT before the block (the enable_pool mux on matrix_size_int), the block, and two LUT levels after it (the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux and the buffer_select_pool0 mux), i.e. three of the original soft levels and their wire hops are gone. Estimated worst remaining path through it, using the table: 0.060 (FF) + 1.273 (wire into soft logic) + 0.954 (mux level) + 2.14 (block) + 1.273 (block into soft logic) + 0.954 + 0.093 (two mux levels) + 2.25 (last wire into the RAM) ≈ 9.0 ns; the xk → bx path through the block is shorter (0.060 + 1.52 + 2.14 + 1.273 + 0.954 + 0.093 + 2.25 ≈ 8.3 ns). Both are below 10.6617, so this stage is no longer expected to set the period.

The one stage the rewrite lengthens is pooling's own x/y/k counter stage, which now also computes xk (one extra 11-bit add next to the existing k+filter_size_int and the two comparators, all inside pooling with short local wires): roughly 0.060 + 1.273 + 3×0.954 + ~1.0 ≈ 6.2 ns, far from critical. No other stage is touched.

Register stages, before and after. For the converted region: matrix_size (cfg), y, x, k are all register outputs and raddr_accum0_pool is combinational from them — 0 register stages from operands to result before the rewrite. After the rewrite the operands are matrix_size, y and xk, all register outputs read in the same cycle, and the result is still combinational — 0 register stages, unchanged. xk is written in the same cycle in which x and k are written, so it adds no latency anywhere; the 15-stage raddr_accum1..15_pool delay line that follows is untouched.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the saturating 8-bit downcast of mul_out_temp in seq_mac (lines 4937-4949, which the source's own TODO at line 4936 proposes removing in favour of truncating once after the accumulation) is what blocks the 256 PE multiply-adds from each becoming one comb_mult_add_16; accumulating the untruncated product would make every PE a direct ax*ay+bx fit, though the device's 132 DSP tiles would still only hold about half of them.

5596	module norm_sub(
5597		input enable_norm,
5598	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
5599	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
5600	    input in_data_available,
5601	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5602	    output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5603	    output out_data_available,
5604	    input  validity_mask,
5605	    input clk,
5606	    input reset
5607	);
5608	
5609	reg out_data_available_internal;
5610	wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5611	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
5612	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
5613	reg norm_in_progress;
5614	
5615	//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
5616	assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available;
5617	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data;
5618	
5619	always @(posedge clk) begin
5620	    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
5621	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5622	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5623	    end else if (in_data_available||norm_in_progress) begin
5624	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5625	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5626	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5627	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5628	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5629	        //this module in 1 clock.
5630	        if (validity_mask == 1'b1) begin
5631	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data - mean);
5632	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data * inv_var);
5633	        end 
5634	        else begin
5635	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data);
5636	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data);
5637	        end
5638	    end
5639	    else begin
5640	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5641	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5642	    end
5643	end
5644	
5645	//The data is normalized in two cycles so we are shifting in_data_available by 2 to generate out_data_available
5646	always @(posedge clk) begin
5647		norm_in_progress <= in_data_available;
5648		out_data_available_internal <= norm_in_progress;
5649	end
5650	
5651	assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;
5652	
5653	endmodule
5654	
5655	//Simple sual port RAM is not understood by VTR

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_mult_add_16 (precision INT16)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx
  • input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.ws.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 94 tiles (qmult 255 of its 256 element products on 84 tiles, 49 of them full; norm_sub 16 on 5 tiles; sub_activation 16 on 6; pooling's 8 x 8 product on 1); the 256th element product and pooling's 8 x 11 address product share the one tile in two_mult_18x19 mode.

qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:4969$853(sum_in=top)#9sop16products registered 0 stage(s) before the sum16 products in one chain of adders
sub_activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:8509$1681mult_add16products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 1
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 9.0
MAPPING: ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int} (pooling line 7489, enable_pool ? matrix_size : 8'b1); ay = {5'b0, y} (pooling's 11-bit counter y, line 7610); bx = {21'b0, xk}, where xk is a new 11-bit register in pooling that holds x + k (see PLAN); result[AWIDTH-1:0] = result[10:0] drives the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool` (line 7612).

PLAN:

What is converted

Only pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133, the one mult_add in
assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff: ((matrix_size_int)*y + x + k); (line 7612).
This is the product that sits on the design's slowest stage: its result runs through the +x, +k adders and the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux to raddr_accum0_pool, out of pooling into top, through the buffer_select_pool0 mux (line 6515, raddr_buffer0 = buffer_select_pool0 ? raddr_accum0_pool : ...) into the accumulator buffer RAM's address input — exactly the reported "five LUTs into a block RAM input". One block absorbs the multiply and the whole +x+k sum, so three of those soft levels and their wires leave the critical stage.

Edit to make

  1. In pooling, add an 11-bit register xk that always carries x + k, maintained in the same always @(posedge clk) block as x, y, k (lines 7614-7632), one assignment per existing branch so it updates, holds and resets exactly when x and k do:

    • if(~resetn|~start_pooling): xk <= 0; (matches new x=0, k=0)
    • else if (y == (matrix_size_int-1) & x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k + filter_size_int; (new x=0, new k=k+filter_size_int)
    • else if (x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k; (new x=0, new k=k)
    • else if (start_pooling): xk <= x + k + 1; (new x=x+1, new k=k)
    • implicit else (hold): no assignment, so xk holds, as x and k do.
      All of these are 11-bit assignments, so they truncate mod 2^11 exactly as x<=x+1 and k<=k+filter_size_int do; xk therefore equals (x+k) mod 2^11 in every cycle after the first reset. x and k themselves are kept unchanged — they are still read by the counter logic (lines 7620-7630), so nothing moves inside the block that is read elsewhere.

  2. Instantiate the block in pooling and take its low 11 bits:

      wire [63:0] raddr_accum0_pool_result;
  comb_mult_add_16 u_raddr_accum0_pool_mult (
      .ax({8'b0, matrix_size_int}),
      .ay({5'b0, y}),
      .bx({21'b0, xk}),
      .result(raddr_accum0_pool_result)
  );
  assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling) ? 11'h7ff : raddr_accum0_pool_result[`AWIDTH-1:0];

    The ~resetn|~start_pooling mux stays outside the block, unchanged, so raddr_accum0_pool is still 11'h7ff whenever the original forces it — this is also what makes the added register safe: on every cycle in which xk could be stale the output does not depend on it.

  3. Change nothing else. raddr_accum0_pool keeps its width, its 15-deep delay pipeline (line 7635) and its port behaviour.

Why this is the same value. All operands are unsigned (matrix_size_int 8-bit, y, x, k 11-bit), and the original RHS is evaluated in the 11-bit context of raddr_accum0_pool, i.e. (matrix_size_int*y + x + k) mod 2^11. The block computes matrix_size_int*y + (x+k) exactly (max 255·2047 + 2047 < 2^32, so the 64-bit result never wraps), and taking result[10:0] reduces mod 2^11; since xk ≡ x+k (mod 2^11), the low 11 bits agree. The block is combinational and has no registers, so no reset has to be derived for it. No ASSUMES: is needed.

Candidates left, and why

  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16) and pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (1): pure multiplies, no sum for the block's bx to absorb. Each one converted costs a whole DSP tile (the block's mode takes a tile alone) in place of a quarter of a tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and removes no soft logic, so area rises and delay is unchanged. :8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int) is already a hard multiply whose fan-out is ordinary flip-flops (pool_count0, pool_norm_valid, pool0..15), not the slowest stage, so converting it buys nothing and costs a tile.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1..#16 (16 ids, 256 products): left for two independent reasons. (a) Correctness/shape: in seq_mac the product does not reach the adder directly — mul_out is a saturating 8-bit downcast of the 16-bit product (lines 4937-4949) and only then added to c_flopped by qadd. (saturate(p) + c) mod 256 is not (p + c) mod 2^64 truncated, so the block cannot hold the multiply and that add together; the downcast would have to stay outside and nothing would be absorbed. (b) Capacity/area: one block per product is 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, and even one 16-product chain would be 16 tiles where the baseline uses 4, with 16 blocks in series (16×2.14 + 15 wires ≈ 51 ns) if the chain were fused. Not viable.
  • sub_activation.$mul$...:8509$1681 (16): the product is registered one stage before the sum (slope_applied_data_internal, line 8509, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so fusing means merging those two registers into one — possible, since both update, hold and reset together and neither is read anywhere else, and the 8-bit truncations compose. But it is left out on cost: it removes only 16 eight-bit adders and 16 eight-bit registers while taking 16 dedicated DSP tiles in place of 16 slots that currently share 6 tiles, roughly +10 tiles ≈ +2.5e6 area (+3.4%) against a saving of a fraction of that in soft logic, and it is nowhere near the slowest stage, so delay does not improve. Net objective would rise.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates 1 block, on 1 DSP tile of its own. The 8×11 product leaves the soft-inferred multiplier pool; the tile it shared in two_mult_18x19 mode is freed and its other occupant (the 256th qmult element product) packs into one of the 37 tiles that are not full in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. So the design uses 93 tiles for the remaining 289 inferred multiplies plus 1 block tile: 94 tiles, unchanged against the baseline's 94, and well inside the device's 132. Used logic-block area is therefore flat to slightly below baseline (two 11-bit adders removed, one 11-bit register added), and routed wirelength is essentially unchanged; the gain in the objective comes from delay.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains more than a single block, so the block contribution to any stage is 2.14 ns, against the 10.6617 ns baseline critical path.

Soft logic that leaves, and which stage. The +x and +k 11-bit adders leave the stage that runs from the matrix_size/y/x/k registers through the multiply to the accumulator-buffer RAM address input — that stage is the design's slowest (10.6617 ns, the reported pooling path: BRAM-side start, one LUT into the multiply, five LUTs into a block RAM input). After the rewrite that stage contains, at most, one LUT before the block (the enable_pool mux on matrix_size_int), the block, and two LUT levels after it (the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux and the buffer_select_pool0 mux), i.e. three of the original soft levels and their wire hops are gone. Estimated worst remaining path through it, using the table: 0.060 (FF) + 1.273 (wire into soft logic) + 0.954 (mux level) + 2.14 (block) + 1.273 (block into soft logic) + 0.954 + 0.093 (two mux levels) + 2.25 (last wire into the RAM) ≈ 9.0 ns; the xk → bx path through the block is shorter (0.060 + 1.52 + 2.14 + 1.273 + 0.954 + 0.093 + 2.25 ≈ 8.3 ns). Both are below 10.6617, so this stage is no longer expected to set the period.

The one stage the rewrite lengthens is pooling's own x/y/k counter stage, which now also computes xk (one extra 11-bit add next to the existing k+filter_size_int and the two comparators, all inside pooling with short local wires): roughly 0.060 + 1.273 + 3×0.954 + ~1.0 ≈ 6.2 ns, far from critical. No other stage is touched.

Register stages, before and after. For the converted region: matrix_size (cfg), y, x, k are all register outputs and raddr_accum0_pool is combinational from them — 0 register stages from operands to result before the rewrite. After the rewrite the operands are matrix_size, y and xk, all register outputs read in the same cycle, and the result is still combinational — 0 register stages, unchanged. xk is written in the same cycle in which x and k are written, so it adds no latency anywhere; the 15-stage raddr_accum1..15_pool delay line that follows is untouched.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the saturating 8-bit downcast of mul_out_temp in seq_mac (lines 4937-4949, which the source's own TODO at line 4936 proposes removing in favour of truncating once after the accumulation) is what blocks the 256 PE multiply-adds from each becoming one comb_mult_add_16; accumulating the untruncated product would make every PE a direct ax*ay+bx fit, though the device's 132 DSP tiles would still only hold about half of them.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_mult_add_16.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.ws.v

response

I'll read the design source to check the plan's claims.

APPROVE

Checked against the source:

  • Converted region raddr_accum0_pool (line 7612): AWIDTH=11, DWIDTH=8, so ax={8'b0,matrix_size_int} (line 7489), ay={5'b0,y} (line 7610), bx={21'b0,xk} are width-legal; the original expression evaluates mod 2^11 and 255*2047+2047 < 2^32, so result[10:0] matches exactly.
  • The new xk branch-by-branch assignments reproduce (x+k) mod 2^11 for all four branches of lines 7614–7632 (branch 4 is always taken when reached, since ~start_pooling is caught by branch 1), and the 11-bit truncations match x<=x+1 / k<=k+filter_size_int.
  • Added-register start-up: start_pooling is an original register reset to 0 (lines 6156–6172) and the testbench holds resetn=0 through its reset phase before comparison begins, so xk is written 0 before any cycle in which line 7612 selects the product; mid-run resetn pulses re-zero x,y,k,xk together.
  • Visibility: x, y, k occur only at 7612 and in their own counter (7616–7630); nothing read elsewhere moves inside the block. raddr_accum0_pool keeps width, the 15-deep pipeline (7635–7649) and its port.
  • Left regions: norm_sub:5632 / pooling:8143 (no sum), qmult:4969 (the saturating downcast at 4937–4949 sits between product and qadd at 4954, so the add cannot be absorbed), sub_activation:8509 (registered one stage before the add at 8511) — all 20 candidate ids accounted for.
  • All required output items are present (blocks=1, tiles=94, one block in series at 2.14 ns vs 10.6617 ns, soft logic leaving the slowest stage, 0→0 register stages).

One citation nit, not a correctness issue: the reader of raddr_accum0_pool in top is line 6500 with select buffer_select_pool, not line 6515 / buffer_select_pool0 (6515 is the raddr_accum15_pool copy).

1	// comb_mult_add_16: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx, with ax, ay and bx unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); the 16x16 product is 32
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every input
6	// reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_mult_add_16 (
8	    input  [15:0] ax,
9	    input  [15:0] ay,
10	    input  [31:0] bx,
11	    output [63:0] result
12	);
13	
14	    wire [31:0] prod = ax * ay;                                      // 16x16 -> 32, exact
15	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx;                                    // zero-extended, exact
16	
17	    assign result = sum;
18	
19	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.ws.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_mult_add_16 (precision INT16)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx
  • input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133 (module pooling, operation mult_add, datatype int11). In the design its products are registered 0 stage(s) before the sum.
  • The sum these products feed also adds terms that are not products: k, x.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.ws.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 1
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 9.0
MAPPING: ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int} (pooling line 7489, enable_pool ? matrix_size : 8'b1); ay = {5'b0, y} (pooling's 11-bit counter y, line 7610); bx = {21'b0, xk}, where xk is a new 11-bit register in pooling that holds x + k (see PLAN); result[AWIDTH-1:0] = result[10:0] drives the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool` (line 7612).

PLAN:

What is converted

Only pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133, the one mult_add in
assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff: ((matrix_size_int)*y + x + k); (line 7612).
This is the product that sits on the design's slowest stage: its result runs through the +x, +k adders and the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux to raddr_accum0_pool, out of pooling into top, through the buffer_select_pool0 mux (line 6515, raddr_buffer0 = buffer_select_pool0 ? raddr_accum0_pool : ...) into the accumulator buffer RAM's address input — exactly the reported "five LUTs into a block RAM input". One block absorbs the multiply and the whole +x+k sum, so three of those soft levels and their wires leave the critical stage.

Edit to make

  1. In pooling, add an 11-bit register xk that always carries x + k, maintained in the same always @(posedge clk) block as x, y, k (lines 7614-7632), one assignment per existing branch so it updates, holds and resets exactly when x and k do:

    • if(~resetn|~start_pooling): xk <= 0; (matches new x=0, k=0)
    • else if (y == (matrix_size_int-1) & x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k + filter_size_int; (new x=0, new k=k+filter_size_int)
    • else if (x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k; (new x=0, new k=k)
    • else if (start_pooling): xk <= x + k + 1; (new x=x+1, new k=k)
    • implicit else (hold): no assignment, so xk holds, as x and k do.
      All of these are 11-bit assignments, so they truncate mod 2^11 exactly as x<=x+1 and k<=k+filter_size_int do; xk therefore equals (x+k) mod 2^11 in every cycle after the first reset. x and k themselves are kept unchanged — they are still read by the counter logic (lines 7620-7630), so nothing moves inside the block that is read elsewhere.

  2. Instantiate the block in pooling and take its low 11 bits:

      wire [63:0] raddr_accum0_pool_result;
  comb_mult_add_16 u_raddr_accum0_pool_mult (
      .ax({8'b0, matrix_size_int}),
      .ay({5'b0, y}),
      .bx({21'b0, xk}),
      .result(raddr_accum0_pool_result)
  );
  assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling) ? 11'h7ff : raddr_accum0_pool_result[`AWIDTH-1:0];

    The ~resetn|~start_pooling mux stays outside the block, unchanged, so raddr_accum0_pool is still 11'h7ff whenever the original forces it — this is also what makes the added register safe: on every cycle in which xk could be stale the output does not depend on it.

  3. Change nothing else. raddr_accum0_pool keeps its width, its 15-deep delay pipeline (line 7635) and its port behaviour.

Why this is the same value. All operands are unsigned (matrix_size_int 8-bit, y, x, k 11-bit), and the original RHS is evaluated in the 11-bit context of raddr_accum0_pool, i.e. (matrix_size_int*y + x + k) mod 2^11. The block computes matrix_size_int*y + (x+k) exactly (max 255·2047 + 2047 < 2^32, so the 64-bit result never wraps), and taking result[10:0] reduces mod 2^11; since xk ≡ x+k (mod 2^11), the low 11 bits agree. The block is combinational and has no registers, so no reset has to be derived for it. No ASSUMES: is needed.

Candidates left, and why

  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16) and pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (1): pure multiplies, no sum for the block's bx to absorb. Each one converted costs a whole DSP tile (the block's mode takes a tile alone) in place of a quarter of a tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and removes no soft logic, so area rises and delay is unchanged. :8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int) is already a hard multiply whose fan-out is ordinary flip-flops (pool_count0, pool_norm_valid, pool0..15), not the slowest stage, so converting it buys nothing and costs a tile.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1..#16 (16 ids, 256 products): left for two independent reasons. (a) Correctness/shape: in seq_mac the product does not reach the adder directly — mul_out is a saturating 8-bit downcast of the 16-bit product (lines 4937-4949) and only then added to c_flopped by qadd. (saturate(p) + c) mod 256 is not (p + c) mod 2^64 truncated, so the block cannot hold the multiply and that add together; the downcast would have to stay outside and nothing would be absorbed. (b) Capacity/area: one block per product is 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, and even one 16-product chain would be 16 tiles where the baseline uses 4, with 16 blocks in series (16×2.14 + 15 wires ≈ 51 ns) if the chain were fused. Not viable.
  • sub_activation.$mul$...:8509$1681 (16): the product is registered one stage before the sum (slope_applied_data_internal, line 8509, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so fusing means merging those two registers into one — possible, since both update, hold and reset together and neither is read anywhere else, and the 8-bit truncations compose. But it is left out on cost: it removes only 16 eight-bit adders and 16 eight-bit registers while taking 16 dedicated DSP tiles in place of 16 slots that currently share 6 tiles, roughly +10 tiles ≈ +2.5e6 area (+3.4%) against a saving of a fraction of that in soft logic, and it is nowhere near the slowest stage, so delay does not improve. Net objective would rise.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates 1 block, on 1 DSP tile of its own. The 8×11 product leaves the soft-inferred multiplier pool; the tile it shared in two_mult_18x19 mode is freed and its other occupant (the 256th qmult element product) packs into one of the 37 tiles that are not full in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. So the design uses 93 tiles for the remaining 289 inferred multiplies plus 1 block tile: 94 tiles, unchanged against the baseline's 94, and well inside the device's 132. Used logic-block area is therefore flat to slightly below baseline (two 11-bit adders removed, one 11-bit register added), and routed wirelength is essentially unchanged; the gain in the objective comes from delay.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains more than a single block, so the block contribution to any stage is 2.14 ns, against the 10.6617 ns baseline critical path.

Soft logic that leaves, and which stage. The +x and +k 11-bit adders leave the stage that runs from the matrix_size/y/x/k registers through the multiply to the accumulator-buffer RAM address input — that stage is the design's slowest (10.6617 ns, the reported pooling path: BRAM-side start, one LUT into the multiply, five LUTs into a block RAM input). After the rewrite that stage contains, at most, one LUT before the block (the enable_pool mux on matrix_size_int), the block, and two LUT levels after it (the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux and the buffer_select_pool0 mux), i.e. three of the original soft levels and their wire hops are gone. Estimated worst remaining path through it, using the table: 0.060 (FF) + 1.273 (wire into soft logic) + 0.954 (mux level) + 2.14 (block) + 1.273 (block into soft logic) + 0.954 + 0.093 (two mux levels) + 2.25 (last wire into the RAM) ≈ 9.0 ns; the xk → bx path through the block is shorter (0.060 + 1.52 + 2.14 + 1.273 + 0.954 + 0.093 + 2.25 ≈ 8.3 ns). Both are below 10.6617, so this stage is no longer expected to set the period.

The one stage the rewrite lengthens is pooling's own x/y/k counter stage, which now also computes xk (one extra 11-bit add next to the existing k+filter_size_int and the two comparators, all inside pooling with short local wires): roughly 0.060 + 1.273 + 3×0.954 + ~1.0 ≈ 6.2 ns, far from critical. No other stage is touched.

Register stages, before and after. For the converted region: matrix_size (cfg), y, x, k are all register outputs and raddr_accum0_pool is combinational from them — 0 register stages from operands to result before the rewrite. After the rewrite the operands are matrix_size, y and xk, all register outputs read in the same cycle, and the result is still combinational — 0 register stages, unchanged. xk is written in the same cycle in which x and k are written, so it adds no latency anywhere; the 15-stage raddr_accum1..15_pool delay line that follows is untouched.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_mult_add_16.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// comb_mult_add_16: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx, with ax, ay and bx unsigned
// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); the 16x16 product is 32
// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
// Ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every input
// reaches result in the same cycle.
module comb_mult_add_16 (
    input  [15:0] ax,
    input  [15:0] ay,
    input  [31:0] bx,
    output [63:0] result
);

    wire [31:0] prod = ax * ay;                                      // 16x16 -> 32, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx;                                    // zero-extended, exact

    assign result = sum;

endmodule
===

        // block enabled (read back after a fixed wait: with activation enabled the control block waits

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_mult_add_16 (precision INT16)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx
  • input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.ws.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 1
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 9.0
MAPPING: ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int} (pooling line 7489, enable_pool ? matrix_size : 8'b1); ay = {5'b0, y} (pooling's 11-bit counter y, line 7610); bx = {21'b0, xk}, where xk is a new 11-bit register in pooling that holds x + k (see PLAN); result[AWIDTH-1:0] = result[10:0] drives the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool` (line 7612).

PLAN:

What is converted

Only pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133, the one mult_add in
assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff: ((matrix_size_int)*y + x + k); (line 7612).
This is the product that sits on the design's slowest stage: its result runs through the +x, +k adders and the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux to raddr_accum0_pool, out of pooling into top, through the buffer_select_pool0 mux (line 6515, raddr_buffer0 = buffer_select_pool0 ? raddr_accum0_pool : ...) into the accumulator buffer RAM's address input — exactly the reported "five LUTs into a block RAM input". One block absorbs the multiply and the whole +x+k sum, so three of those soft levels and their wires leave the critical stage.

Edit to make

  1. In pooling, add an 11-bit register xk that always carries x + k, maintained in the same always @(posedge clk) block as x, y, k (lines 7614-7632), one assignment per existing branch so it updates, holds and resets exactly when x and k do:

    • if(~resetn|~start_pooling): xk <= 0; (matches new x=0, k=0)
    • else if (y == (matrix_size_int-1) & x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k + filter_size_int; (new x=0, new k=k+filter_size_int)
    • else if (x==(filter_size_int-1)): xk <= k; (new x=0, new k=k)
    • else if (start_pooling): xk <= x + k + 1; (new x=x+1, new k=k)
    • implicit else (hold): no assignment, so xk holds, as x and k do.
      All of these are 11-bit assignments, so they truncate mod 2^11 exactly as x<=x+1 and k<=k+filter_size_int do; xk therefore equals (x+k) mod 2^11 in every cycle after the first reset. x and k themselves are kept unchanged — they are still read by the counter logic (lines 7620-7630), so nothing moves inside the block that is read elsewhere.

  2. Instantiate the block in pooling and take its low 11 bits:

      wire [63:0] raddr_accum0_pool_result;
  comb_mult_add_16 u_raddr_accum0_pool_mult (
      .ax({8'b0, matrix_size_int}),
      .ay({5'b0, y}),
      .bx({21'b0, xk}),
      .result(raddr_accum0_pool_result)
  );
  assign raddr_accum0_pool = (~resetn|~start_pooling) ? 11'h7ff : raddr_accum0_pool_result[`AWIDTH-1:0];

    The ~resetn|~start_pooling mux stays outside the block, unchanged, so raddr_accum0_pool is still 11'h7ff whenever the original forces it — this is also what makes the added register safe: on every cycle in which xk could be stale the output does not depend on it.

  3. Change nothing else. raddr_accum0_pool keeps its width, its 15-deep delay pipeline (line 7635) and its port behaviour.

Why this is the same value. All operands are unsigned (matrix_size_int 8-bit, y, x, k 11-bit), and the original RHS is evaluated in the 11-bit context of raddr_accum0_pool, i.e. (matrix_size_int*y + x + k) mod 2^11. The block computes matrix_size_int*y + (x+k) exactly (max 255·2047 + 2047 < 2^32, so the 64-bit result never wraps), and taking result[10:0] reduces mod 2^11; since xk ≡ x+k (mod 2^11), the low 11 bits agree. The block is combinational and has no registers, so no reset has to be derived for it. No ASSUMES: is needed.

Candidates left, and why

  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16) and pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (1): pure multiplies, no sum for the block's bx to absorb. Each one converted costs a whole DSP tile (the block's mode takes a tile alone) in place of a quarter of a tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt, and removes no soft logic, so area rises and delay is unchanged. :8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int) is already a hard multiply whose fan-out is ordinary flip-flops (pool_count0, pool_norm_valid, pool0..15), not the slowest stage, so converting it buys nothing and costs a tile.
  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1..#16 (16 ids, 256 products): left for two independent reasons. (a) Correctness/shape: in seq_mac the product does not reach the adder directly — mul_out is a saturating 8-bit downcast of the 16-bit product (lines 4937-4949) and only then added to c_flopped by qadd. (saturate(p) + c) mod 256 is not (p + c) mod 2^64 truncated, so the block cannot hold the multiply and that add together; the downcast would have to stay outside and nothing would be absorbed. (b) Capacity/area: one block per product is 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, and even one 16-product chain would be 16 tiles where the baseline uses 4, with 16 blocks in series (16×2.14 + 15 wires ≈ 51 ns) if the chain were fused. Not viable.
  • sub_activation.$mul$...:8509$1681 (16): the product is registered one stage before the sum (slope_applied_data_internal, line 8509, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so fusing means merging those two registers into one — possible, since both update, hold and reset together and neither is read anywhere else, and the 8-bit truncations compose. But it is left out on cost: it removes only 16 eight-bit adders and 16 eight-bit registers while taking 16 dedicated DSP tiles in place of 16 slots that currently share 6 tiles, roughly +10 tiles ≈ +2.5e6 area (+3.4%) against a saving of a fraction of that in soft logic, and it is nowhere near the slowest stage, so delay does not improve. Net objective would rise.

Blocks and tiles. The rewrite instantiates 1 block, on 1 DSP tile of its own. The 8×11 product leaves the soft-inferred multiplier pool; the tile it shared in two_mult_18x19 mode is freed and its other occupant (the 256th qmult element product) packs into one of the 37 tiles that are not full in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode. So the design uses 93 tiles for the remaining 289 inferred multiplies plus 1 block tile: 94 tiles, unchanged against the baseline's 94, and well inside the device's 132. Used logic-block area is therefore flat to slightly below baseline (two 11-bit adders removed, one 11-bit register added), and routed wirelength is essentially unchanged; the gain in the objective comes from delay.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains more than a single block, so the block contribution to any stage is 2.14 ns, against the 10.6617 ns baseline critical path.

Soft logic that leaves, and which stage. The +x and +k 11-bit adders leave the stage that runs from the matrix_size/y/x/k registers through the multiply to the accumulator-buffer RAM address input — that stage is the design's slowest (10.6617 ns, the reported pooling path: BRAM-side start, one LUT into the multiply, five LUTs into a block RAM input). After the rewrite that stage contains, at most, one LUT before the block (the enable_pool mux on matrix_size_int), the block, and two LUT levels after it (the ~resetn|~start_pooling mux and the buffer_select_pool0 mux), i.e. three of the original soft levels and their wire hops are gone. Estimated worst remaining path through it, using the table: 0.060 (FF) + 1.273 (wire into soft logic) + 0.954 (mux level) + 2.14 (block) + 1.273 (block into soft logic) + 0.954 + 0.093 (two mux levels) + 2.25 (last wire into the RAM) ≈ 9.0 ns; the xk → bx path through the block is shorter (0.060 + 1.52 + 2.14 + 1.273 + 0.954 + 0.093 + 2.25 ≈ 8.3 ns). Both are below 10.6617, so this stage is no longer expected to set the period.

The one stage the rewrite lengthens is pooling's own x/y/k counter stage, which now also computes xk (one extra 11-bit add next to the existing k+filter_size_int and the two comparators, all inside pooling with short local wires): roughly 0.060 + 1.273 + 3×0.954 + ~1.0 ≈ 6.2 ns, far from critical. No other stage is touched.

Register stages, before and after. For the converted region: matrix_size (cfg), y, x, k are all register outputs and raddr_accum0_pool is combinational from them — 0 register stages from operands to result before the rewrite. After the rewrite the operands are matrix_size, y and xk, all register outputs read in the same cycle, and the result is still combinational — 0 register stages, unchanged. xk is written in the same cycle in which x and k are written, so it adds no latency anywhere; the 15-stage raddr_accum1..15_pool delay line that follows is untouched.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the saturating 8-bit downcast of mul_out_temp in seq_mac (lines 4937-4949, which the source's own TODO at line 4936 proposes removing in favour of truncating once after the accumulation) is what blocks the 256 PE multiply-adds from each becoming one comb_mult_add_16; accumulating the untruncated product would make every PE a direct ax*ay+bx fit, though the device's 132 DSP tiles would still only hold about half of them.

response

I'll read the design source and supporting files.

ACCEPT

The edit matches the approved plan region-for-region and nothing outside it changed.

  • Plan conformance. Only pooling's raddr_accum0_pool region (lines 7609–7644) is touched: the 11-bit xk register (line 7612), the block instance (7614–7619), and the rewritten assign (7620). The ~resetn|~start_pooling mux, raddr_accum0_pool's width/port (7433), and the 15-deep delay line (7646–7662) are untouched. Only one comb_mult_add_16 instance exists in the file.
  • Ports. All three inputs are connected by name with matching widths: ax={8'b0, matrix_size_int} (8-bit, line 7489) → 16; ay={5'b0,y} (AWIDTH=11) → 16; bx={21'b0,xk} → 32; result is a 64-bit wire whose low AWIDTH bits drive the non-reset mux arm, as the plan states.
  • Same behavior. xk is updated in every branch of the same always block as x/k, and each assignment matches the new x+k: reset → 0 (x=0,k=0); branch 2 → k+filter_size_int (x=0, k'=k+fs); branch 3 → k (x=0, k held); branch 4 → x+k+1. The implicit hold branch is unreachable whenever resetn & start_pooling (branch 4's guard is start_pooling), so xk ≡ (x+k) mod 2^11 after every edge, including after a mid-run reset. All assignments truncate to 11 bits exactly as the originals do. The block's exact 8×11 product plus xk (max 255·2047+2047 < 2^32) never wraps at 64 bits, and result[10:0] reproduces the original 11-bit assign context (matrix_size_int*y + x + k) mod 2^11. An X on the mux select merges the same two arms as before.
  • Added register. xk starts unknown, but on any cycle it could be stale ~resetn|~start_pooling holds and the mux forces 11'h7ff; start_pooling (original register, 0 at start, line 6156) covers power-up, and xk is driven to 0 on the first edge.
  • Visibility/terms. x, y, k remain outside and are still read by the counter logic; no value read elsewhere moved inside the block. No assumption is needed, consistent with the plan's "No ASSUMES:".

1	// comb_mult_add_16: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx, with ax, ay and bx unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); the 16x16 product is 32
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every input

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.ws.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_mult_add_16 (precision INT16)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx
  • input ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.ws under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 10.6617 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 94 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 7.45611e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 381872, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 3.03568e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in pooling: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (pooling's), from a block RAM output through one LUT into the multiply, then five LUTs into a block RAM input; wire 7.96 ns in all, 1.31 of it into the multiply. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 94 tiles (qmult 255 of its 256 element products on 84 tiles, 49 of them full; norm_sub 16 on 5 tiles; sub_activation 16 on 6; pooling's 8 x 8 product on 1); the 256th element product and pooling's 8 x 11 address product share the one tile in two_mult_18x19 mode.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • The block's internal delay is fixed. Soft logic between a block's output and the next register adds to that stage; blocks in series each add their delay plus a wire.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
