response

I'll read the design source before planning.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 87
STAGE_NS: 9.90586
MAPPING: no new block is instantiated; the kept first-pass mapping stands unchanged (ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int}, ay = {5'b0, y}, bx = {21'b0, xk}, result[10:0] -> the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool at line 7612)
PLAN:

Decision: no further rewrite. The first-pass candidate (pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133 on one comb_mult_add_16) is the best available with this block and should be kept: 1 block, 87 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, slowest stage 9.90586 ns, product 2.67325e14 (-11.94% vs baseline). The header above describes that kept design; this plan adds no block to it.

Why no further candidate is worth converting.

The stage that now sets the period is not the stage the kept block is in. The seed-1 report shows the critical path running from a flip-flop, through one LUT, into a design multiply cell ($mul~6627, 2.140 ns), then through five LUT levels into a flip-flop: 9.93 ns, of which 6.67 ns is wire. The kept block does not appear on it (my block's path is a register or config bit, one block at 2.14 ns, one output mux, then the RAM address: about 7.4 ns by the table's medians, well under 9.91). So any further gain must come from the stage of one of the design's remaining multiplies, and a block cannot shorten any of them: the block's input-to-output delay (2.14 ns) is exactly the multiply delay it would replace, so substituting it leaves the stage identical unless it also absorbs the soft logic around it.

  • qmult.$mul...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1 through #16 (256 products, 16 chains): left. What the sum adds is not the product but a saturating 8-bit downcast of it (lines 4937-4949: the result depends on mul_out_temp[15], on the OR of bits 14:7, and on bits 6:0, and is not the product's low bits - e.g. products in 32768..32895 take the {1, p[6:0]} branch). No block port can produce that value, so the block cannot carry the product into the qadd; all the saturate and add soft logic would stay, leaving the stage the same length. Independently, one block per product would need 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, so these cannot be converted at all. The five LUT levels after the multiply on the measured critical path match this saturate-plus-add shape, so even the most likely critical stage is untouchable by this block.
  • pooling.$mul...:8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int, one shared instance feeding the 17 pool_countN == fs*fs comparisons and pool_norm_valid): left. It is a bare product with no sum, so a block would absorb no soft logic, and it already holds a DSP tile; the conversion is delay-neutral (2.14 ns for 2.14 ns), area-neutral at best and +1 tile (+253779, about 9 CLBs) if the packer currently shares its tile with qmult products. Its own downstream (a 16-bit equality against an 8-bit counter, then the select/increment) cannot be folded into ax*ay+bx: forming fs*fs - pool_count in the block only turns a 16-bit equality into a 33-bit zero test, which is deeper, and would need one block per comparator (17 tiles). Nothing to win, something to lose.
  • norm_sub.$mul...:5632$876 (16 products): left. "Products reach no sum" - a bare multiply between two registers, so the block absorbs no soft logic and the stage keeps the same 2.14 ns. The 16 products sit four to a tile on 5 tiles today; as blocks they would take 16 tiles alone, +11 tiles = +2.79e6 min-width transistor areas, about +3.8% area, for zero delay change. Strictly worse on the objective.
  • sub_activation.$mul...:8509$1681 (16 products): left. The product is registered one stage before its sum (line 8509 into slope_applied_data_internal, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so a T2 regroup would have to move that register and would make intercept_applied_data_internal appear one cycle early - a behaviour change, and the register is also re-zeroed by the reset/idle branches at 8497 and 8544. Even if regrouped, each block absorbs only one 8-bit add (about 1 CLB each, 16 CLBs total) while costing 16 tiles against the 6 these products share today: +10 tiles = +2.54e6, about +3.5% area, against about 4.5e5 of soft logic saved. Worse.
  • pooling.$mul...:7612$1133: already converted and kept (see below). No better variant exists: moving the output mux ((~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff : ...) onto the ax/bx inputs only trades one LUT after the block for one before it, and splitting the sum over two blocks in series would cost a second tile and 2 x 2.14 ns plus a wire in one stage.

Blocks and tiles. The design keeps the single block of the first pass; no block is added. DSP tiles: 87 as packed at seed 1 (baseline 94).

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one, 2.14 ns, in the pooling read-address stage. That stage measures well below the slowest stage (9.90586 ns), which contains a design multiply plus five LUT levels and which the block does not touch.

Soft logic moved, and which stage. In the kept rewrite the 11-bit + x + k adder left the pooling read-address stage (the block now forms matrix_size_int*y + xk), which leaves only the reset/start mux between the block and the RAM address input; that stage is not the slowest stage, and the stage that is slowest is unchanged by the rewrite. The counter stage gained the xk register and its adder (about 2 CLBs); it is short (x, k registers through one 11-bit add into a register) and is not the slowest stage.

Register stages for the converted region, before and after. Before: matrix_size_int is combinational from a configuration register, y, x and k are registers, the product and the + x + k sum are in the same cycle as the address (0 register stages between the product and its sum), and raddr_accum0_pool is consumed in that same cycle. After: unchanged - ax comes from the same configuration register, ay from y, bx from the new xk register that holds the same x+k the original adder formed in that cycle, and result[10:0] drives the same mux in the same cycle; still 0 register stages between the product and the sum, and no register was added to or removed from the address path.

No assumption is needed: the kept rewrite matches the original for every value of matrix_size, filter_size, enable_pool and start_pooling (the testbench writes the configuration registers at random, so none may be assumed stable), and the reference simulation passed.

DESIGN_CHANGE: pooling recomputes filter_size_int*filter_size_int into a 16-bit equality against 17 shift-chain copies of pool_count (lines 7495, 7607, 7678-8143) inside one stage. Computing that product, or the comparison itself, once into a register and pipelining the comparison alongside pool_count1..16 would take the multiply and most of those comparators out of the stage entirely - the single largest remaining delay item in the design - and would also let the product be dropped or moved off its DSP tile. It cannot be done as a rewrite here because the configuration registers that feed filter_size can change on any cycle, so registering the product changes what the design computes.

2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); the 16x16 product is 32
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[16], ay[16], bx[32] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every input

5596	module norm_sub(
5597		input enable_norm,
5598	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
5599	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
5600	    input in_data_available,
5601	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5602	    output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5603	    output out_data_available,
5604	    input  validity_mask,
5605	    input clk,
5606	    input reset
5607	);
5608	
5609	reg out_data_available_internal;
5610	wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5611	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
5612	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
5613	reg norm_in_progress;
5614	
5615	//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
5616	assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available;
5617	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data;
5618	
5619	always @(posedge clk) begin
5620	    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
5621	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5622	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5623	    end else if (in_data_available||norm_in_progress) begin
5624	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5625	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5626	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5627	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5628	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5629	        //this module in 1 clock.
5630	        if (validity_mask == 1'b1) begin
5631	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data - mean);
5632	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data * inv_var);
5633	        end 
5634	        else begin
5635	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data);
5636	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data);
5637	        end
5638	    end
5639	    else begin
5640	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5641	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5642	    end
5643	end
5644	
5645	//The data is normalized in two cycles so we are shifting in_data_available by 2 to generate out_data_available
5646	always @(posedge clk) begin
5647		norm_in_progress <= in_data_available;
5648		out_data_available_internal <= norm_in_progress;
5649	end
5650	
5651	assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;
5652	
5653	endmodule

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 7.90%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 7.90%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

The plan, in full 7139 chars · not approved

I'll read the design source before planning.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 87
STAGE_NS: 9.90586
MAPPING: no new block is instantiated; the kept first-pass mapping stands unchanged (ax = {8'b0, matrix_size_int}, ay = {5'b0, y}, bx = {21'b0, xk}, result[10:0] -> the non-reset branch of raddr_accum0_pool at line 7612)
PLAN:

Decision: no further rewrite. The first-pass candidate (pooling.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:7612$1133 on one comb_mult_add_16) is the best available with this block and should be kept: 1 block, 87 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, slowest stage 9.90586 ns, product 2.67325e14 (-11.94% vs baseline). The header above describes that kept design; this plan adds no block to it.

Why no further candidate is worth converting.

The stage that now sets the period is not the stage the kept block is in. The seed-1 report shows the critical path running from a flip-flop, through one LUT, into a design multiply cell ($mul~6627, 2.140 ns), then through five LUT levels into a flip-flop: 9.93 ns, of which 6.67 ns is wire. The kept block does not appear on it (my block's path is a register or config bit, one block at 2.14 ns, one output mux, then the RAM address: about 7.4 ns by the table's medians, well under 9.91). So any further gain must come from the stage of one of the design's remaining multiplies, and a block cannot shorten any of them: the block's input-to-output delay (2.14 ns) is exactly the multiply delay it would replace, so substituting it leaves the stage identical unless it also absorbs the soft logic around it.

  • qmult.$mul...:4969$853(sum_in=top)#1 through #16 (256 products, 16 chains): left. What the sum adds is not the product but a saturating 8-bit downcast of it (lines 4937-4949: the result depends on mul_out_temp[15], on the OR of bits 14:7, and on bits 6:0, and is not the product's low bits - e.g. products in 32768..32895 take the {1, p[6:0]} branch). No block port can produce that value, so the block cannot carry the product into the qadd; all the saturate and add soft logic would stay, leaving the stage the same length. Independently, one block per product would need 256 DSP tiles on a 132-tile device, so these cannot be converted at all. The five LUT levels after the multiply on the measured critical path match this saturate-plus-add shape, so even the most likely critical stage is untouchable by this block.
  • pooling.$mul...:8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int, one shared instance feeding the 17 pool_countN == fs*fs comparisons and pool_norm_valid): left. It is a bare product with no sum, so a block would absorb no soft logic, and it already holds a DSP tile; the conversion is delay-neutral (2.14 ns for 2.14 ns), area-neutral at best and +1 tile (+253779, about 9 CLBs) if the packer currently shares its tile with qmult products. Its own downstream (a 16-bit equality against an 8-bit counter, then the select/increment) cannot be folded into ax*ay+bx: forming fs*fs - pool_count in the block only turns a 16-bit equality into a 33-bit zero test, which is deeper, and would need one block per comparator (17 tiles). Nothing to win, something to lose.
  • norm_sub.$mul...:5632$876 (16 products): left. "Products reach no sum" - a bare multiply between two registers, so the block absorbs no soft logic and the stage keeps the same 2.14 ns. The 16 products sit four to a tile on 5 tiles today; as blocks they would take 16 tiles alone, +11 tiles = +2.79e6 min-width transistor areas, about +3.8% area, for zero delay change. Strictly worse on the objective.
  • sub_activation.$mul...:8509$1681 (16 products): left. The product is registered one stage before its sum (line 8509 into slope_applied_data_internal, added to data_intercept_delayed at line 8511), so a T2 regroup would have to move that register and would make intercept_applied_data_internal appear one cycle early - a behaviour change, and the register is also re-zeroed by the reset/idle branches at 8497 and 8544. Even if regrouped, each block absorbs only one 8-bit add (about 1 CLB each, 16 CLBs total) while costing 16 tiles against the 6 these products share today: +10 tiles = +2.54e6, about +3.5% area, against about 4.5e5 of soft logic saved. Worse.
  • pooling.$mul...:7612$1133: already converted and kept (see below). No better variant exists: moving the output mux ((~resetn|~start_pooling)? 11'h7ff : ...) onto the ax/bx inputs only trades one LUT after the block for one before it, and splitting the sum over two blocks in series would cost a second tile and 2 x 2.14 ns plus a wire in one stage.

Blocks and tiles. The design keeps the single block of the first pass; no block is added. DSP tiles: 87 as packed at seed 1 (baseline 94).

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one, 2.14 ns, in the pooling read-address stage. That stage measures well below the slowest stage (9.90586 ns), which contains a design multiply plus five LUT levels and which the block does not touch.

Soft logic moved, and which stage. In the kept rewrite the 11-bit + x + k adder left the pooling read-address stage (the block now forms matrix_size_int*y + xk), which leaves only the reset/start mux between the block and the RAM address input; that stage is not the slowest stage, and the stage that is slowest is unchanged by the rewrite. The counter stage gained the xk register and its adder (about 2 CLBs); it is short (x, k registers through one 11-bit add into a register) and is not the slowest stage.

Register stages for the converted region, before and after. Before: matrix_size_int is combinational from a configuration register, y, x and k are registers, the product and the + x + k sum are in the same cycle as the address (0 register stages between the product and its sum), and raddr_accum0_pool is consumed in that same cycle. After: unchanged - ax comes from the same configuration register, ay from y, bx from the new xk register that holds the same x+k the original adder formed in that cycle, and result[10:0] drives the same mux in the same cycle; still 0 register stages between the product and the sum, and no register was added to or removed from the address path.

No assumption is needed: the kept rewrite matches the original for every value of matrix_size, filter_size, enable_pool and start_pooling (the testbench writes the configuration registers at random, so none may be assumed stable), and the reference simulation passed.

DESIGN_CHANGE: pooling recomputes filter_size_int*filter_size_int into a 16-bit equality against 17 shift-chain copies of pool_count (lines 7495, 7607, 7678-8143) inside one stage. Computing that product, or the comparison itself, once into a register and pipelining the comparison alongside pool_count1..16 would take the multiply and most of those comparators out of the stage entirely - the single largest remaining delay item in the design - and would also let the product be dropped or moved off its DSP tile. It cannot be done as a rewrite here because the configuration registers that feed filter_size can change on any cycle, so registering the product changes what the design computes.

No schematic pair for this rewrite.

Pass 1: area spread 0.0, period spread 0.5208 ns, wirelength spread 4008 across the seeds.
